# History of candidates, runs and repairs

This is the running log that lived in `AGENTS.md` until October 31, 2026, newest first, kept word for word. The current working notes for agents are in `AGENTS.md`; the design canon is `docs/DESIGN_DOCUMENT.md`.

November 3 candidate 0.4.297, Drisking and the fourth visit (docs/BORRASCA_TOWN_0_4_297.md). Eli and Wren meet the reader before they go missing. The Sheriff patrols the refinery, checks the shutdown and can be made to yield. CI for 0.4.296 stopped at a punctuation error in the gate log. Corrected here.

November 3 candidate 0.4.296, Borrasca gate and Sheriff (docs/BORRASCA_GATE_0_4_296.md). The padlock sits on the near face of the gate. The beam starts at the Sheriff’s flashlight lens. He returns the reader downhill if they refuse to leave. The gate hut sheet opens as a gate log.

November 3 candidate 0.4.291, service hazards and staircase testing (docs/SERVICE_HAZARDS_0_4_291.md). Return Air, Settling Cellar, operator test sheets and a supported Great Staircase arrival. Layout 91.

November 3 candidate 0.4.288, reading and reconnecting (docs/READING_AND_RECONNECT_0_4_288.md). Johnny keeps his current exchange for the reader to reopen after a disconnect. The private story cards offer manual page turns and hold that reader's transition until Done. Protocol 128.

November 3 candidate 0.4.287, the plain in the Sahel (docs/PLAIN_SAHEL_0_4_287.md; owner: "Consider actual regional landscaping and architecture"). PlainSahel.dress, called at the end of Landscapes.plain: redden() turns sand, sandstone, smooth sandstone and sandstone slabs (slab type kept) into their red forms over x -46..46, z -180..16, y -6..12, except the vestibule (|x| <= 10, z >= 0); ground() (|x| <= 20, z -62..-3) lays coarse dirt where Landscapes.noise(2x,2z,173) < -0.45 and dead bushes (5%) or short grass (1%) on open red sand; seven termite mounds (red sandstone spires two or three high with a red sandstone wall tip, a lump and a slab at the foot) at |x| 13..19; two thorn trees (acacia, leaning trunk, flat crown radius 3, a side-limb crown) at (-14,-31) and (16,-13). Landscapes.dune() also knows the red blocks. ScenePolish.border: a red sand top gets red sandstone under it, and the plain's far ridge is red. The dunes keep every rise two or more. No Witness, layout, protocol or story change: 74 / 56 / two kinds / three endings.

November 3 candidate 0.4.284, Camp Blood in the Pine Barrens (docs/CAMP_BLOOD_BARRENS_0_4_284.md; owner: "Consider actual regional landscaping and architecture"). SceneCraft: Tree gains PITCH and SCRUB (other scenes' mixes stop at SNAG); Camp Blood's grounds run barrensFloor() before woods(4, {0,0,20,10,5,50,15}). pitchPine: spruce log 8-12 high, crown rings r2/r2/r1 at height-1..height+1, two or three log arms from y 4 up each ending in a needle tuft, a trunk sprout on some. SCRUB: oak log 1-2 high under rings r1/r2/r1. barrensFloor: top podzol at y -1 becomes coarse dirt where Landscapes.noise(seed 131+ordinal) < -0.2, and sand where a second noise < -0.62 in the woods (not open(), not buildingClearing, not within 3 of a story position). ground() accepts sand at Camp Blood. undergrowth() at Camp Blood: fallen trunks at h >= 988, otherwise barrensCover (bracken, grass, moss, dead bush, oak-leaf scrub as a feature; dead bush or grass on sand; only crown grass on the road at x 0); no boulders. cabins(): foundation course polished andesite, chimneys bricks with a brick wall cap. campGround(): fire ring packed mud and granite. LiteraryRooms.camp(): the service road (x -2..2, z -8..-98) is sand, not gravel. ScenePolish.border at Camp Blood: pitch pines (pitchPine, 8-11 high) at distance >= 2, one- or two-high oak-leaf scrub, ferns and grass; the far wall 8-10 high, oak leaves to y+3 and spruce needles above. No cabin, door, bed, paper, actor, cover node, hunt route or story position moves. No Witness, layout, protocol or story change: 74 / 56 / two kinds / three endings.

SecondPersonStreet.dress, called at the end of SecondPersonSite.build, inside the room (y up to 12): the near terrace's parapet becomes a deepslate-tile eave (stairs facing south at y 10, z 0), chimney stacks (brick y 10-11, flower pots at 12) at seven party walls, smooth-stone heads at y 3 and 8 over every window and door, brick at y 4-5 so the first-floor windows are y 6-7; a two-storey house each side of the launderette (x -16..-10 and 9..16, z -12..-18, brick to y 7, iron door and fanlight, windows y 1-2 and 5-6 with heads at 3 and 7, a gable roof with eaves at 8 and ridge at 10, a chimney stack with pots at 12) and walled, flagged back yards behind them (z -19..-31, brick walls two high, two cauldrons); two chimney stacks on the launderette's flat roof (x -9/7, z -21); house fronts at both road ends (x +/-17, z -1..-11: door and fanlight at z -6, windows with heads, an eave, a chimney stack) with a kerb across the road's end (x +/-16, z -7..-4); the backs of the next street on x +/-17 beyond the yards (back windows, an eave, a chimney stack); an iron trapdoor grille round the street tree. SecondPersonSite.season leaves the crown's eight corners open. No doorsteps: the architecture pass allows nothing to climb in a doorway. No Witness, layout, protocol or story change: 74 / 56 / two kinds / three endings. November 3 candidate 0.4.282, Johnny Truant's second slice (docs/TRUANT_0_4_282.md; owner: "Then finish truant"; the draft, docs/TRUANT_RESIDENT_DESIGN_DRAFT.md). Truant.Turn (HELP, CLAIM, DOORWAY, COVER, SHOVE) is chosen once per meeting by choose(): the target is the present reader with the most meetings who is collecting (deepest >= 20, Twenty noted, and meeting > TwentyAt); the first collecting meeting is DOORWAY at the held door and CLAIM elsewhere (Revealed); then HELP 4 / CLAIM 3 / the place's hostile form 3 in 10, after harm (HarmAt > ForboreAt) HELP 7 / CLAIM 3; SHOVE only with HELP_STEADY, after Revealed, and 5 meetings past ShovedAt, else CLAIM; the gate (any present reader with deepest < 20) turns every hostile form into CLAIM and stops one under way. CLAIM: help as before; the target's panel (claim()) reads his papers (TruantTexts.page, twelve first-person retellings from the reader's Answers) at the lamp or from worn 2, says his claim (TruantTexts.claim: the_rest / come_back / maybe_was_yes / owed_for_help / my_places, kept in Claims with its meeting, never written over Promise or Help), asks the collector's question about a told place, and offers correct / tell new / retell / challenge / limit; then a favour (go_back, cross_alone, lamp_out) kept in Favours with answer and Did. The half smile leans out at the lamp (lamp stage 4, "You skipped something."); at his lamp he holds a page (gesture 18). DOORWAY: in the doorway (IN_DOORWAY) with the leer and a hand on the frame (19), door held open; the claim opens on approach with "Not yet."; paying steps him aside; the first push into the doorway is shoved back (Struggled, harm doorway), the second gives way. COVER: at the lamp within 2.6, the lantern is put out (darken, saved under Dark, relit when due, when the place empties or at ServerStopping), blindness 80, across the path, slowness 6 for 40 with "You first. Then tell me." (Covered, harm cover), released, then the claim. SHOVE: waits at SHOVE_WAIT; when the target is past LEDGE_NEAR-1.5 on the ledge he comes (stone step, footsteps, storeroom breath within 3), and is stopped by the target looking at him for 4 ticks ("Easy. I've got you."), stepping off the ledge, a friend within 1.6 (Interrupted), a blow or the gate (Stopped/StoppedBy); else after 24 ticks the steadying hand for 6, then push (gesture 15) with velocity (-.8,.32,0) (Shoved, ShovedAt, harm shove; Witnessed for the others), look over (16), "Careful.", back to SHOVE_WAIT. Help after harm: open() for a harmed reader says what he did and ends with no question or promise (ForboreAt, AfterHarm, HisPromise); at the ledge after a shove he walks two back, hands off, and still catches. Worn lines (TruantTexts.worn) at ordinary meetings past twenty. Gestures 15-19 in CastModel.truant; LiteraryActor gestures now clamp at 31. No layout, protocol, Witness or story change: 74 / 56 / two kinds / three endings. TruantTests.pastTwentyHeClaimsCollectsAndShovesButStillHelps (exploration, batch truant_after) and the first slice's case pass locally.

November 3 candidate 0.4.281, Proofrock in Idaho (docs/PROOFROCK_IDAHO_0_4_281.md; owner: "Consider actual regional landscaping and architecture"). Nothing inside the town's room changes level: ProofrockTown.mountains() builds slopes in the nine rows past the west, north and east edges and behind the town for |x| > 12 (out to the builder's prepared bounds); slope(x,z,out) is out-1 for the first two rows (a step at a time, within OutdoorBounds' walkable two-row margin) and 1+1.3d+.12d^2 plus a sine wobble past them, capped at 12; columns from y -5, granite where a neighbour differs by two or more, IdahoShore.floor on top, boulders, fern and grass past row two; IdahoShore.tree on a jittered 3-grid in rows 3..6 (aspen likelier along the lake). ProofrockTown.tree(): lodgepole and aspen in town (crowns from y 3-4, radius 2, as the old spruce), Douglas fir as well in the eastern woods (x >= 40), aspen by the water. ground(): IdahoShore.shore on the north beach west of x -38 and the bay shore z -80..-34. Run 1415 Borrasca ladder fix is its own commit. No Witness, layout, protocol or story change: 74 / 56 / two kinds / three endings. Locally 23 of 24 town cases pass (architecture pass, DrownedTownTests, LakeAmbushTests, StreetlightTests, the town StaceyTests, the church originals case); DrownedTownTests.nativeWitchDetoursAroundGrassAndCannotBePushedIntoWater, which builds its own fixture, fails locally with or without this release and last passed in CI run 1413.

November 3 candidate 0.4.279, Winter Lake in Idaho (docs/WINTER_LAKE_IDAHO_0_4_279.md; owner: "Consider actual regional landscaping and architecture"). WinterLake: bank(x,z) is 0 on the near bank (z >= -11) and rises with distance past x +/-28 or z -73 (rounded at the far corners, the foot wandering by a sine), d*.9+d*d*.09 capped at 7 and tapered for z > -17; the ground fill builds each column to that height, snow block and a snow layer on top, granite (IdahoShore.rock) where a neighbour differs by two or more. trees(): IdahoShore.tree(..., winter=true) on a jittered 3-grid over |x| >= 30 or z <= -75, granite boulders on the slopes and at the ice's edge along the side and far banks (never the near bank), then IdahoShore.snowCap on every bank column. ArchitectureTests.export cuts Costume Night, Movie Night and Winter Lake at 17 instead of 7, so the Idaho banks' trees show whole. No Witness, layout, protocol or story change: 74 / 56 / two kinds / three endings. The literary namespace (190 cases) passes locally; the view was rendered.

November 3 candidate 0.4.277, Borrasca in the Ozarks (docs/BORRASCA_OZARKS_0_4_277.md; owner: "Consider actual regional landscaping and architecture. Drisking Missouri"). No position, walk, sightline, story or text change. BorrascaLand: top() is an oak-hickory floor (grass, leaf drifts as coarse dirt, rooted dirt high up, moss only in the wettest hollows; no podzol anywhere); rock() dolomite with diorite chert (no tuff); the gully bed chert gravel with stone ledges and stone-stair risers; cut banks earth() with terracotta red clay; forest() plants tallOak (oak, or dark oak hickory; clean trunk, crown from seven up), pine as shortleaf (open rounded crown, no spire), sycamore (birch trunk, oak crown, white limbs) within nine of the gully, cedar (mangrove trunk, narrow spruce-leaf column) in the open; only tall trees by the cliffs; fallen trunks oak and dark oak, the one across the gully birch; the clearing's rim grass, its west side cedars, its stump and saplings oak. BorrascaRooms.walked edges coarse dirt and grass. BorrascaDressing: PRUITT birch under waxed weathered copper (green tin), HOLLIS a rock house (wallCell: mud bricks, packed mud, granite, cobble) under polished andesite (galvanised), WEBB stripped oak under deepslate tile, CORLEY brick under waxed exposed copper (rusted tin) with a brick chimney; stripped birch corner boards and birch porch posts on frame houses; porch roofs in the house's roofing; chain-link (iron bars) along the street with an opening and the name post; redbud (cherry leaves) and dogwood (flowering azalea) in two yards; the church floored in oak under polished andesite with an andesite steeple top, board FIRST BAPTIST CHURCH; poles dark oak; trees() tall oaks, oaks and cedars; the Triple Tree oak with oak leaves (BorrascaTown.fort's burl, trunks, limbs and crowns too); the depot heap grey chat (gravel, andesite, stone, diorite). BorrascaTown: the hedgerow oak leaves on an oak rail fence, two tall oaks behind; store porch oak under rusted tin; lot stops oak; fort floor coarse dirt. BorrascaTests: the fort's trunk is OAK_LOG. No Witness, layout or protocol change: 74 / 56 / two kinds / three endings. The Borrasca namespace (21 cases) and the architecture pass pass locally; the five Borrasca views were rendered and checked.

November 3 candidate 0.4.275, the listener's questions answered from memory (docs/CONFESSION_0_4_275.md; owner: "Might be worth revising the confession vignette to use the new dialogue tree, house memory, and personhood vignettes"). ConfessionAnswers asks each chapter's question in the Talk panel (key confession_<chapter>, speaker "The listener"); the offers are saved with the question (Offers) so a closed panel or a restart reopens it, and LiteraryVignettes.talk resumes it. labyrinth/ConfessionMemory builds them from the reader's own record: HouseReadProse clauses (first quiet room, in the first person), HouseReadWardrobe coat / waiting-room item / bathroom routine, ThreeFingers.lostCompanion, TalkHistory speakers and the reader's own replies, the four kill stats. A true answer is written as said; an untrue one is written with "(Under it, in another hand: ...)" where the House knows better (Corrections list, sealed pages, quill transcript); "Say nothing." is always last and never corrected; no memory, no correction. Typed chat still answers, uncorrected. The account adds a line counting the corrections (LiteraryTexts.confessionCorrected). Talk marks chosen replies and TalkHistory keeps them as R. No layout, protocol or Witness change: 74 / 56 / two kinds / three endings. LiteraryTests: the confession case answers in the panel (one true, one corrected, one silence, the rest by chat); new theListenersAnswersComeFromWhatTheHouseRemembers.

SecondPersonStory writes every line, the book's latest page and the account; SecondPersonPayload (network 127) gives the client the flat's hour (SceneClock mode 15). Witness 74 / 56 / two kinds / three endings. SecondPersonTests (literary): a summer to the cupboard; an autumn evening through to the account. ArchitectureTests checks the flat.

November 1 candidate 0.4.218: the replacement (docs/REPLACEMENT_0_4_218.md), the owner's October 31 idea. Slot 145, literary, outside (scene clock 9), multi-visit, understanding; Witness 69 / 52 / two kinds / three endings (WitnessPool 69 / 52 / 46). The first moment the reader isn't looking at their following companions (after 20 ticks), they are held in the pen (STAY, sitting, invulnerable, tagged) and an owner-bound actor (new skin literary_replacement, stranger phase 43, crawling) is at their heels making the companion's noises (pitched cat or wolf sounds, action-bar lines), catching up behind them when left more than nine blocks and out of sight. Opening the crate releases them (FOLLOW) and the replacement stands, says the name, and goes to lie on the front room's blanket; back on the street (z >= -50) with every released companion within 16 blocks readies took_it_home and puts a page in the mailbox at (2,0,-3), the account (the ledger page and the reader's words). Leaving with a companion still held sends it to the manor's front hall, sitting (LeftBehind218); no account that visit. Witness line, House of Leaves place, co-op lines, miniature, advancement account/replacement. Layout 71. Four ReplacementTests; the architecture case checks the street.

November 1 candidate 0.4.215: the household's last rooms (docs/STAIRCASE_HOUSEHOLD_0_4_215.md). Four more household doors (StaircaseHousehold.room): the long way home (DRIVE: the passenger seat, passing lamps, the radio, the fogged windscreen at the lights, leaning by looking into the bend, the junction's map or a wrong turn to the sheep, the handbrake twice; Jonah's note), washing day (YARD: four shirts on the line, the blanket's other end, the sparrows, then 500 ticks of rain to get it all in and the clothes horse; Sabine's), the lay-by (LAYBY: sandwiches, the wrapping folded, three passing tractors watched, back in the seat; the horn wakes Bell; Mara's) and the last landing (LANDING: the household's camp; count the cups and spoons, the lamp, the candle cut, the morning list with the reader's own name last in their own hand; a crouching hand keeps something and fails it). The landing door opens only in the bottom stretch, on a staircase descent, after three household rooms (leaks or doors) and until the household has moved on; success sets MovedOn and resolves the new understanding story household (moved_on). Walking out of any room now captions what the household does behind the reader. 67 / 51 / two kinds / three endings (WitnessPool 67 / 51 / 44). Advancement account/household. November 1 candidate 0.4.213: your own light (docs/OWN_LIGHT_0_4_213.md), the last of the five dark-hall horrors. Slot 143, layout 69, one new understanding story: 66 / 50 / two kinds / three endings. Dealt only from depth sixteen (DarkHalls.horror); built whole in the dark halls' ash by OwnLightSite (a lobby 9 wide with words scratched into its west wall; two branches 3 wide side by side from z -5 to -44 with a 3-thick wall between broken by gaps a block wide and two high every six blocks; the far room z -45 to -49 with the way on and sixteen wall-torch places; a burnt stub on its floor). Entering a branch starts the run; a private double (the lookout's LOOKOUT_DOUBLE role, now walking with its legs and holding a torch through an ItemInHandLayer) keeps level across the wall from a 40-tick trail, mirrored: ten ticks behind, then from z -12 ten ticks ahead (extrapolated), crouching and turning as the reader does; every torch or lantern the reader places is recorded (Torches213) and mirrored across the wall. Past halfway (z -25) it stops matching, steps through the gap behind them into their branch and walks back at .13 a tick to the lobby and out through the way in (SLAM), its own torches out at once and the reader's taken down with the dark halls' RUMBLE as it passes within 1.6. Coming back six blocks after it turned is following: every torch removed, rumble, slam, darkness, back at the fork, no far-room ending that visit. Reaching the far room otherwise takes anything left, sets that many wall torches back in placing order and readies the account (let_it_take_them). Either way Home213: next time within 12 of their respawn point the nearest chest has its slots reversed, lit candles and campfires within 10 go out and a note in their own hand ("Thank you for the light." or "Not yet.") lies on the bed. The peers-see-you-a-beat-late client effect is not built. The source is the scratched BRING YOUR OWN LIGHT / it will bring its own; the account, "The account: the light", pastes the note in. Co-op, miniature, staircase prose, co-author and advancement lines; art (scratch, stub) in the shared horror tool; no new sounds. Five OwnLightTests in the literary namespace; WitnessPool 66 / 50 / 44.

November 1 candidate 0.4.212: the line (docs/LINE_0_4_212.md), the fourth of the five dark-hall horrors, after Exploration #4. Slot 142, layout 68, one new survival story: 65 / 49 / two kinds / three endings. Dealt only from depth sixteen (DarkHalls.horror); built whole in the dark halls' ash by LineSite (a short hall to a doorframe; past it a space 49 wide, 80 deep and 6 high with two-by-two columns on a fixed hash, never within 3.2 of the line; the line, a fixed polyline of about 92 blocks at hand height from the frame to the pack; the tag on the knot, the pack and recorder at the end, the way on behind them). Taking the tag after reading it holds the line (Holding212): its glimmer is sent as particles to that reader alone within 2.4 blocks of the hand; more than 1.8 off it or sprinting lets go, with blindness until the hand finds it again within .9 (not while running). A third of the way along the line tugs from behind (twice, three seconds apart, then steadily); the thing then comes along the line toward the nearest hand at .09 a tick, .16 while that hand stands still, its weight pulling every hand toward it and, within 4, dragging hard with half a heart of the_line damage a second (never below two hearts), the view dipping. Reaching a hand (.9) reels it in: blindness, three returns consumed and a sendBack (depth lost), the line wound round the arm (a string that cannot be dropped, gone on waking) and one ordinary stack taken (Tied212), returned tied to the pack the next time they reach it. Reaching the pack first readies the tape (held_on) and slackens the line for everyone. Co-op: every hand feels the tugs; a holder at the door pulls the tag twice to warn the others (Warned212). The source is the tag's rules ("If it pulls twice, it isn't"); the account, "The account: the tape", is a transcript in the reader's own hand dated four days on from finding it, ending with the pack. Co-op, miniature, staircase prose, co-author and advancement lines. Art (tag, pack, recorder) and four subtitled cues in the shared horror tools, checked in CI. Five LineTests in the literary namespace; WitnessPool 65 / 49 / 43.

November 1 candidate 0.4.211: the stillness (docs/STILLNESS_0_4_211.md), the third of the five dark-hall horrors: the growl made flesh. Slot 141, layout 67, one new survival story: 64 / 48 / two kinds / three endings. Dealt only from depth sixteen (DarkHalls.horror); built whole in the dark halls' ash by StillnessSite (a hall 11 wide, 3 high and 48 long, square pillars at x ±3 every six blocks, the surveyor's log page by the way in, the tally before the far door, which is the way on). Past halfway (z -24) the growl comes from the far door and the thing walks the hall, unseen: the Minotaur's hoofs and bellow, dust, a screen shake (novel-scene mode 21) and cloven HOOF prints placed in the dust every stride and where it stands; both doors are held while it is there (retreatLocked). It hunts by sound (still 0, crouch 3, walk 10, jump 14, run 24, food/drop/door 48 blocks), going to a sound at .30 a tick and otherwise to the nearest reader it has not stood over by smell at .10, and stands over the reader it reaches for 20 to 40 seconds: a counted breath every three seconds with a fog of it on their screen, a shove with its head every four and a half to seven seconds, the heartbeat climbing to a shake. Moving (beyond .35 blocks after a half-second grace, its own shoves excepted), jumping or a loud noise under it is a goring: a new stillness damage type through armour and the grace after joining, 30 (a real House death) or, through a raised shield, 4 with a Minotaur block counted on the shield; a survivor is thrown back out the way they came (LabyrinthDoors.sendBack). When everyone in the hall has been stood over it walks out through the far door, the hoofs fading; a reader it stood over who then reaches the far door readies the count (let_it_breathe). Co-op: one sound draws it to all; another reader's noise draws it off whoever it stands over (spared and counted) to the one who made it. The source is an unsigned survey page; the account, "The account: the count", is the reader's own count of breaths and shoves, with the times they moved and who drew it off whom. Co-op, miniature, staircase prose, co-author and advancement lines. Art (log, tally, hoof) and four subtitled cues in the shared horror tools, checked in CI. Five StillnessTests in the literary namespace; WitnessPool 64 / 48 / 42.

November 1 candidate 0.4.210: the storeroom (docs/STOREROOM_0_4_210.md), the second of the five dark-hall horrors, after Johnny's storeroom. Slot 140, layout 66, one new survival story: 63 / 48 / two kinds / three endings. Dealt only from depth sixteen (DarkHalls.horror); built whole in the dark halls' ash by StoreroomSite (a hall 3 wide and 20 long with Johnny's footnote just inside and the way on in its east wall; a dark oak door on a spring at its end; the storeroom 12 deep and 4 high between steel shelving, one bulb on a pull chain, boxes; the list beside the door on its inside). The list (the source) is in the reader's own hand: black ink, needles, and a thing of theirs, copied once from their copied home's containers, their ender chest or what they carry (else a tripwire hook), always an everyday thing (no containers, enchantments, rarity, precious metals or gems), with their name on a tag. The room runs once the door has banged shut behind a reader in it. The shared dread fills in 30 s, 15 with a back to the door, at half rate with two or more inside, with the chain swinging, breathing nearer, a heartbeat and darkness at the edges; facing the door empties it, but once the list is read each look (and every 4 s held) kills the bulb for a second and brings the side shelves a block nearer (5, 3, 1 wide, then shut), pushing readers into what is left. From the second look the pinch hurts a heart every 3 s and spends breath fast; at the third the shelves meet and kill (new storeroom damage type through armour and the grace after joining). The dread full: a hand on the shoulder, the head turned to the door against the mouse, the bulb out, a three-heart blow, and the reader comes to in the hall: one ordinary stack gone for good, the three things taken back, the tagged thing returned to their bed three days later (or their pocket two days after that); it is never copied twice. All three and backing into the door without facing it: the door opens behind them and the footnote is ready (did_not_turn_round). The account, "The account: the footnote", is Johnny's footnote running off its page into the reader's own hand. Co-op, miniature, staircase prose, co-author and advancement lines. Art (11 more textures and models in the shared horror_fitting block, now with a stage property for the bulb) and six subtitled cues, checked in CI. Six StoreroomTests in the literary namespace; WitnessPool 63 / 48 / 41.

November 1 candidate 0.4.209: the closing hall (docs/CLOSING_HALL_0_4_209.md), the first of the five dark-hall horrors the owner approved ("the most extreme yet"; "real deaths are the right call"). Slot 139, layout 65, one new survival story: 62 / 47 / two kinds / three endings. Dealt only from depth sixteen (DarkHalls.horror); a one-shot room in the dark halls' ash, built whole by ClosingHallSite (hall 7 wide, 6 high, 46 long; the squeeze west through its wall at z -3; the room past it with Karen's interview, the way on and a passage north). Chad and Daisy Navidson (stranger phases 39 and 40, child scale, new painted skins) wait at the far end; reading Daisy's drawing starts it for everyone in the hall: the way in sealed, the squeeze cracked open, the walls and ceiling stepping in over nine stages (four seconds apart alone, the last after six; twice as fast with two readers), each with a grind and the growl under it. Children come only while held (a hand at the heel; a second on the back, at a crawl's pace); the hall shoves and hurts readers in its way and crushes them at the last stage (a new closing_hall damage type through armour and the grace after joining: a real House death), but never closes on a child (it waits up to five seconds, then closes round them). In the squeeze a child goes first; a reader who goes in ahead loses the child. Going back for the last child at the last stage pushes them through and shuts the hall on the reader. Both through and the reader after: sent_them_through; dying for the last: went_back (read later at the copy by the way in); a child left: the visit fails, the child calls the reader's name from the wall, and the first door of every later visit to the dark halls has a small handprint on its inside until both are brought out. Karen's interview names the reader and never their gender. tools/draw_horror_art.py (one horror_fitting block for all five), tools/generate_horror_audio.py (six cues), two skins. Seven ClosingHallTests; the Witness counts every case pins now live in gametest/WitnessPool. Also fixed from run 1377: the Left-Right Jubilation case counts its own window's hold from clean (a random dormer could hold the reader first), and the Mapping father's set-out from the utility room is not counted as a step of his walk.

November 1 candidate 0.4.208: the dark halls, the owner's third series of ordinary halls ("staircase offered at say, 22, and have dark halls range from 16 and start being the only halls at 21"; "the house should occasionally take torches ... with a low rumble, separate from the Minotaur's own"; "Minotaur roars more frequent in deep halls, but not excessively so"). Five gray slots 134-138, layout 64, no protocol or Witness change. DarkHalls builds them in ash-grey materials (tools/draw_dark_hall_art.py), unlit and unpolished: the long hall (104 blocks, ceiling 3 to 8), the forking hall (three branches, one through), the ash maze (11x11 cells, fixed seed, two ways out), the spiral (round a core, a block down every six steps on stairs, under itself twice, out ten down; floor 14 up in its slot) and the edge (a railless walkway beside a 13-block drop; floor 14 up). Dealt from depth 16 (ramping), the only ordinary halls from 21 (stone halls weigh nothing there), the edge only from 20; LabyrinthPacing.STAIRCASE_DEPTH 22 (was 20). The House: about one dark hall in three, never two running, it takes one light the reader set there that is 10+ blocks off and faced by nobody within 128 blocks, no drop, with its own rumble; the growl's mean gap there is 2.5 minutes (min 75 s) against about 4 in the stone halls, and a far schedule is pulled in on entry; at most one echo a reader every four dark halls (footsteps behind in the long hall and spiral, a late door slam), and at the edge a thrown thing is gone and heard landing late two times in three; a reader over the edge is put back at its start unhurt. Four subtitled sounds (tools/generate_dark_hall_audio.py). Four DarkHallTests in the exploration namespace; the first local run found a faced torch 54 blocks off counted unseen (the cone stopped at 48; it now reaches 128). Canon: docs/DARK_HALLS_0_4_208.md.

November 1 candidate 0.4.207: the God of the Pitt, the owner's third proposal of 31 October, as Ted the Caver's deeper visits. Slot 133, layout 63, protocol 114; no Witness story of its own (still 61 sources / 46 required / two kinds / three endings): dealt only to a reader with the Ted Caver resolution, finished for good when its field journal is read. Built whole at its own height (PittCave.BASE_Y 200, found by its real bounds like the lookout): Brad's staging ledge under a sinkhole (his card on a piton, the source; his crate with hammer, carbide lamp and barometer; Brad at the belay, his lamp signalling the draft), Floyd's Tomb (an 18-block crawl under pinch-ceiling slabs, four seams, bolts, a fissure into an unlit parallel crawl), the fist-sized hole, the Wind Tunnel, the Round Stone Room with its stone in copper grooves, two chimneys (the first pinches out; the second, behind the stone, three drops on ropes and three squeezes), the Pre-Echo Ledge over a void with invisible bridges to a rusted ladder and a hatch in the moor, where the field journal waits at first light (scene clock mode 14). One shared draft cycle (14 s draft, 3 s warning, 6 s still air); in the Tomb's still air moving, swinging or a lit lamp steps the slab over a reader down a stage a tick, pins them at the sixth and hurts them until the draft lifts it; at their last heart they black out to the ledge instead. The Seam-Crawler's eyes come along the fissure as the draft dies and its hand reaches at movement or light. Visits: the seams cut only in the draft and the fist hole; the orange line clipped bolt by bolt to the round room and the collapse that follows the reader back along it; Brad gone, the stone pushed out from inside, the first chimney choked, his helmet strobing, his line frayed into a crack; the ledge, the reader's own footprints two steps ahead and their steps heard early, falls caught, the hatch from below, the moor (climbed_out). A second reader can take the belay from Brad and haul a pinned partner out, sharing the beat. The journal is four logs with letter surveys, from what the reader did; Brad's card ties to R.'s line from the caver notebook. Art (60 textures, 70 models and states) and eight cues drawn and generated with --check in CI; seven PittTests in the literary namespace. The first local run found a blackout in the collapse counting as an escape, and the journal case reading through the click instead of the fixture's read. Canon: docs/GOD_OF_THE_PITT_0_4_207.md.

November 1 candidate 0.4.206: the Dead Air Broadcast, the owner's second proposal of 31 October, built after the Left-Right Game. Slot 132, layout 62, protocol 113; Witness 61 sources / 46 required / two kinds / three endings. A 1983 basement inside a black studio: the receiver and the NetNostalgia printouts (the source), the Zenith in snow, Percy the cut-string marionette with his brass marquee, a bulkhead. The dial tunes the hallway past it whole (DeadAirSet.tune): static (paneled, locked), 088.1 the stage (velvet corridor, strings, waves, the end room and its chest), 074.5 the marsh (a flooded drain onto the salt marsh relay: pools, boardwalks, masts Alpha/Beta/Gamma with dishes, pole breakers, cyan bulbs, the shack); it will not leave a channel while anyone is out in it. The Skin-Taker steps toward the nearest reader every four ticks, arms spanning the hallway; its touch puts the reader out to do the visit again. A wall-breaker or pole breaker kills the air for eight seconds: cyan, the tall one frozen solid, crawled under on hands and knees; feedback brings the picture back. Visits: the tube from the chest fitted to the receiver; the three dishes turned to the night man's bearings (90/225/315) until three cyan locks open the shack; the walls down to flats and the teleprompter with the reader's own actions as stage directions; the booth behind the back curtain, a highchair facing nine monitors of snow, the centre one putting the reader on the air (on_the_air) and giving the owner-bound dead-air breaker (eight seconds unseen by sight-hunters, two minutes' cooldown). The set and air are shared; tube, dishes, crawl, script and account are personal. Art and seven cues drawn and generated with --check in CI; six DeadAirTests in the literary namespace; the Witness counts are 61 / 46. The first local run found the receiver retuning without saving the channel; it saves after every retune. Canon: docs/DEAD_AIR_0_4_206.md.

November 1 candidate 0.4.205: the Left-Right Game, the owner's first proposal of 31 October. Slot 131, layout 61, protocol 112; Witness 60 sources / 45 required / two kinds / three endings. Outside at two in the morning: the Bristol Ferry toll (the teletype with Apollo's rule, the CB, the convoy log, a crate of flares), an approach between Jersey barriers and sodium lamps, a fork under a sign gantry, two branches into the fog, and the salt cul-de-sac behind the trees. Left, then right, never the same way twice; the CB gives each night's first side. The right branch carries the reader back onto the approach to the next fork; the wrong one leaves them on the salt, where Marjorie moves only while nobody holds her in sight, and backing out across its edge with her held makes it a fork again; turning away, she lunges (a lesson on the first night, the night's end after). Nights: the toll (four forks), the Bluejay cut (a chained gate, the key in a flooding phone box down the wrong branch, a flare on the divider to walk it, Bonnie backwards on the line), Jubilation (doorless pastel houses whose lit dormers slow a reader until they look down at the yellow line), the sinkhole (two forks, then Alice's idling wagon, whose tailgate readies the account). One shared road and sequence for everyone on it; one can hold her while another backs the party out. Apollo's last flare, kept, lights again anywhere in the House. Seven LeftRightTests in the literary namespace; art and seven cues with --check in CI. Canon: docs/LEFT_RIGHT_0_4_205.md.

November 1 candidate 0.4.204: the Polaroid Trail, the owner's fourth proposal of 31 October, built after Work print. Slot 130, layout 60, protocol 111; Witness 59 sources / 45 required / two kinds / three endings. Outside at dusk: the back yard and creek, the culvert and its grate, the crawlspace under the house next door (crawled), a tunnel to the neighbourhood cinema, and the pine wood with two trenches and a deer blind. An unseen watcher's camera glints amber while its flash charges, then flashes; the photograph (the House's own ray-cast camera, the reader from where he stood) is left half a minute later under a yellow balloon. Standing where it was taken and looking back at the camera opens the next way: the culvert grate, the crawlspace hatch (cat bowl, then the bed of stolen clothes), the fire exit (four flashes from the booth while the one behind comes a few rows nearer, then turning round), and in the pines the last photograph from straight above trench A and the reader looking straight up into fifty lit balloons. Looking at the flash puts the reader back on the porch with the visit to do again and photographs them from behind in ordinary halls until they finish. The album resolves it (understanding); Josh's walkie-talkie points the way back. Six PolaroidTrailTests in the literary namespace; art and seven cues with --check in CI. Canon: docs/POLAROID_TRAIL_0_4_204.md.

November 1 candidate 0.4.203: Work print, the cursed film the owner approved on October 30 with three notes (the costume found in the shop, the ghost girl only an Easter egg, a way to succeed and a way to fail). Slot 129, layout 59, protocol 110; Witness 58 sources / 44 required / two kinds / three endings. A closed video rental shop: a tape with no label in the returns bin, the rental card with the reader's own name on it, a back room with two decks and a CRT, an edit bay and a set behind it. The tape watched through starts a seven-House-day count on the shop's phone; from the second viewing the film holds the reader's own footage, taken from behind in halls they walked by a server-side ray-cast camera (HouseLens). It is stopped by passing a dubbed copy to another reader (or a villager in the Overworld) or by finishing the film: the turn of scene 88 shot in the costume on the set's mark, spliced in and run on the flatbed. Run out, the film takes the reader from an ordinary hall onto the set and follows them privately until they stop it. Five WorkPrintTests in the literary namespace; art and nine cues drawn and generated with --check in CI. Canon: docs/WORK_PRINT_0_4_203.md.

November 1 candidate 0.4.202: the Whistlers in the Pines, the owner's fifth proposal, built first (slot 128, layout 58; Witness 57 sources / 43 required / two kinds / three endings): a fire lookout over four nights where a look toward the whistling wall while its blind is up brings the thing in the woods a tier nearer, and the fourth night's forest of towers is left by locking the fire finder on the north one. The owner's November 1 notes on the vignettes' architecture are done in the same candidate: the Barretts' house rebuilt as a family of four's home (one living room, their own photographs, double windows, two characterful bathrooms, the girls' rooms, the linen closet, lines on every family object), Mapping the Interior's own furniture, the floor plan on its table and carpet that is the floor, a real sleeping pose, the Winchester's doorway steps and storeroom and court, Devil's Rock's floating props and one-page diary sheets, Zampanò's readable wall. Two new architecture checks (nothing small hangs in the air; nothing to climb inside a doorway) found and fixed the elk yacht's cabin, the Three Fingers doorstep and Borrasca's store and office steps. Canon: docs/WHISTLERS_0_4_202.md and docs/OWNER_NOTES_0_4_201.md.

Six counted per reader outside the manor (TornPages.counts uses HouseLayout.isInsideDomesticVolume), a seventh returns the oldest; the writer reclaims from the stub (JournalTearPayload back), by crouch-use, crouch-interact or a blow; anything burnt or expired goes home; /ghosthouse journal clearpages <player> (JournalCommands). October 30 rubbings candidate 0.4.179, step 3 of docs/JOURNAL_DESIGN.md (owner's idea 2, one-click pencil rubbings). journal/Rubbings: twelve block kinds already in the rooms (gouges cell_gouges, well_initials well_carvings, sealed_window, scribble vignette_detail SCRIBBLE_*, room_number hotel_room_plaque, town_sign, bark borrasca_bark, ward_notice institute_sign, pasted_paper archive_paper, funeral_plate harrigan_finish PLAQUE, cave_marks, maker_mark streetlight), each with one observation line that never genders the reader nor names a colour or an age (tested). Journals.rub: the reader's own current journal only (else the usual open line, NOT_YOURS); once per kind per reader (Rubbed map saved in the book; a second try opens at that page, TURNED); placed at (.08,.55) with a per-kind tilt on the last written page if it has no rubbing and <=180 chars / <=8 lines, else the next page with that room (FULL if none); stamps an unbegun page; charcoal scratch sound; opens the journal at that spread (JournalOpenPayload carries Spread; protocol stays 100). Journals.rubbing: RightClickBlock at LOWEST priority with receiveCanceled, so the rooms' own handlers (cave marks, well initials, the funeral plaque) answer first; cancels with SUCCESS on both sides when the hand holds a journal and the block is rubbable, so the journal does not also open at its first page; every other right-click still opens the journal. JournalPage gains Rubbings (Rubbing id/x/y/angle; unknown ids dropped on load; fromReader never carries them; withWriting keeps them; empty() counts them). JournalPageRenderer.rubbings draws each 52 px square, tilted, with its line at .65 scale beside it in graphite; JournalScreen and page() draw them under the writing. Art: tools/draw_journal_art.py draws twelve 64x64 charcoal rubbings (textures/gui/rubbing/<id>.png): charcoal on raised surfaces in a diagonal tooth, gaps where things are cut in. Pigeonholes, school desks and the notice post open menus of their own and are left out. Tests: JournalTests.eachAuthoredObjectGivesOneRubbingAndASecondTryTurnsToIt and rubbingsOutlastTheReadersOwnEditsAndAReload (892 declared). Build job: native-journal-3.png (a spread with two rubbings; no missing-texture magenta) before the twelve courtyard views. Run 1344 (ce0fecf, 0.4.178): sixteen of seventeen jobs passed, the build job with 890 structure tests, both drawer cases and the twelve courtyard views among them; live-multiplayer's uncanny preflight lost both readers seven seconds into the tunnel sprint after a 10.5 s host stall (chunks promoted to ticking within view distance mid-sprint, settling generated cave water; the catch-up ticks moved her while the readers' movement could not); UncannySocketProof now leases the tunnel's view-distance corridor asynchronously, waits in phase 4 for it to be loaded and ticking (NativeTestChunks.ticking) and for 40 consecutive ticks at real-time pace, and releases it after phase 19; the tunnel itself is unchanged. Run 1345 (2782452): leasing the whole view-6 corridor at once held the host past the clients' timeout in phase 4 (50 s behind); the uncanny-only preflight now runs at view distance 3 (tools/run_live_expedition.py) and leases only the reach the room's own lease does not keep ticking (two chunks either side and past the end, about 60 chunks), keeping the ticking and pace waits; locally the phase-4 backlog fell from about 40 s to 16.6 s and the sprint passed. Its build job ran all 892 structure cases; the one failure was the Last Stop bar-stool case timing out at 300 ticks waiting for the lot's cast, which is spawned once the lot's bounded build completes (its sibling cases wait 900 to 2400), so that case now waits 900 ticks and names what is still pending. October 29 owner playtest notes, candidate 0.4.163 (owner: "have you implemented the check for "human" skins yet? my player skin is still displaying"; "remove the player character from the inventory menu while in the house"; "house navigation needs to be desperately balanced. I encountered the "deep interior" - the stone passages - before even finding/finishing the personality/personhood vignettes"; "expand or reset the stone pass requirement"; "swing textures are still disconnected"; the carved rail). The House's body is built (HouseBodies, HouseBodyClient, PlayerInfoSkinMixin): each client checks its own skin's face with the hat layer (a quarter in a YCbCr skin box; default skins 44%+, mobs 0%) and sends only yes/no (HouseBodyReportPayload); for an inhuman skin inside the House beyond the manor, the body from the read (AGE age, settled SELF clothes, HAIR cut incl. the new coils; tone/hair from the account's default skin, commonest lower-face and crown colours) is sent to every client (HouseBodiesPayload) and replaces the skin everywhere a player is drawn; 45 painted sheets in tools/paint_character_skins.py with skin/hair masks recoloured additively; read.houseBodies option; README privacy exception; protocol 91. InventorySelfMixin leaves the inventory figure empty in House dimensions. Navigation: while quiet rooms are owed every door of the hall after one leads to the next (chances retired); crossings then are held waypoints counting no depth, a reader already deep is held below STORY_DEPTH, and entering the last room holds the whole stack (LabyrinthData.holdReturns) so stories begin six and stone seven crossings later; returnCount gives the raw stack. Porch: the swing seat is one plank at sitting height hung on its ropes; the carved rail is a white end post with a carved top rail reaching the next rail's post, open below for the lawn's view; quiet-room layout 163. Tests: two new quiet-room cases (36); the tailoring proof makes B inhuman and checks the body on both clients and an empty inventory figure; the art proof gains porch assemblies and ten bodies (22 images). No layout/Witness change outside the quiet rooms: 54 / 41 / two kinds / three endings. Canon: docs/HOUSE_BODY_AND_NAVIGATION_0_4_163.md.

October 24 reworks, candidate 0.4.121: the fifth batch of the owner's notes, built in parts after each plan was approved. Part one. Miniatures: a touch on a model stand shows its card, the reader's name and the room and only what the House recorded of them there (visits, stay, reading, their own outcome, how it ended) in the third person with no pronoun guessed; after twenty seconds the reader's own client may put the mother face down in an upper corner already at the edge of sight and takes her away when looked toward (client-only; skin literary_miniature_mother on the shared grid). Confession: after each dictated chapter the listener asks one question; the reader answers in chat, cancelled from public chat, seen only by them with the fragment he repeats; thirty seconds' silence goes down as unanswered; the sealed journal is his questions and their answers; the resolution is unchanged. Karen's room: the reader's client projects their own F2 screenshots and occasional unannounced 160x90 snapshots (newest 24 in the_oldest_house/karen) on the wall; nothing leaves the computer; client settings projectScreenshots and secretSnapshots, both on. Phone in the canoe: a viewfinder while filming, moonlight over the steeple, the tumble and splash, the moon from under the water, a heartbeat fade, coming to soaked; the recovered phone plays its clip back as drawn night pictures (crouch for the record). Part two. Poltergeist: a 1980s Cuesta Verde tract house built whole (tract_house block, 26 kinds) over three visits: stacking chairs behind a locked child's door; static that opens the door behind its watcher; the tree breaking in on whoever enters the child's room. The Wheel: six numbered rooms as a daughter's life (nursery and childhood room at twice the size, the reader a child going forward; teenage room, first flat, her own daughter's nursery, hospital room), built whole by WheelHouse (life_stage block, 60 kinds); each door is numbered for the room it opens on; going back keeps full height and the child's chair is drawn small for that reader only; room six's 1 goes round to the nursery until the reader has gone back and seen all six, then opens on Mother's room, where they are small, and her unnumbered door lets them out grown; one short text per room. Camp Blood: Camp Wanaki's third session never ended for its director; a shared CampSession clock runs the posted schedule's six 45-second periods (flagpole, mess hall, west row, east row, fire circle, rounds to the road), the bell and PA mark each and his walk follows it; the mess-hall bell calls him there once per reader; under a bunk moving within six blocks gives a reader away and his boots are heard passing; papers: the schedule, three counselors' journals, letters home in footlockers, the nurse's log, the unsigned incident report; a mess hall, flagpole and PA horn; camp_session block (9 kinds); the road ending also opens to a reader who read the schedule or rang the bell. Owner's fix list: the fridge corridor's lamps, hooks and units (and other hazard halls' lamps) sit under the ceiling or on a wall; Goatman speaks before he knocks, knocks far less, crawls only through the sash and is a cousin once inside, the cousins draw back, dialogue checks the moment; the paw's thumb stays still and three more first wishes go wrong; the yacht no longer shows the last room's doorway, blows spray blood, both slashers strike every 75 ticks, portholes break by hand; the drag rides the ground, a second killing blow on one crossing is a death, he keeps away while the reader is buried and thirty seconds after, the pile slumps open after a minute; the child's room bed is low, one doll, a hand on the desk opens the card and starts the room, Dinnerbone returns. Part four, the Winchester house (WinchesterHouse; no generic pass): damask wallpaper on parquet; thirteen steps into the ceiling; a door onto the wall and one high onto nothing; leaded windows onto wallpaper and one in the ledger's floor; the blue séance room under the ledger (table, one chair, thirteen robes); a clock that strikes thirteen privately at 600 visit ticks; private carpenters' hammer and saw behind the nearer wall; between visits an empty, unwatched house swaps which two side-passage doors open onto wall and moves the sawhorse. The switchbacks, ledger and resolution are unchanged. winchester_fitting (8 kinds), 15 textures (tools/draw_winchester.py), three subtitled sounds. Part five, the Barretts' house (BarrettHome, called by GhostsSetRooms): the soundstage round the filming rooms becomes the rest of the family's home (front hall with the stairs, the garage the crew use, the den as video village, laundry, pantry, master bedroom, bathrooms, John's office, spare room); painted walls, two storeys (STOREY: wood floor over a plastered ceiling), blinds on every outside wall, the crew's cables, softboxes and chairs through it; every filming room, mark and route unchanged and walked by a native test. barrett_home (46 kinds), 74 textures (tools/draw_barrett_home.py); owner follow-up: the big ground-floor rooms are furnished along their walls (front room, living room, kitchen table, sideboard, family computer, back-window corner), off every route, Marjorie's crawl and the den door. Part six, Borrasca (BorrascaTown): the same four visits on the same marks; the reader arrives in County Trails' station, with Drisking General (the town's noticeboard on its porch) and the Sheriff's office round the lot; the fort is walled and roofed in a Triple Tree grown from one burl, with Kimber's notes inside; the ridge depot has an office, a sorting room and a back room on one aisle, the machine in its own shed and a gate hut, all off the taking-to-lamp line. Rewritten trail notice, trail map, a board that changes each visit, Kimber's notes (water-stained later) and the property receipt. Five cast skins (tools/draw_borrasca_cast.py), 13 block textures and redrawn trail tiles (tools/generate_borrasca_trail_assets.py). 52 sources / 39 resolutions / two kinds / three endings. Canon: docs/REWORKS_0_4_121.md.

October 24 elk carcasses, candidate 0.4.120: the fourth batch of the owner's notes, textures rendered and reviewed. The cave mouth's pad eases down a block at a time so the trail walks straight in. His killing blow starts the drag: pulsing blackout, a private camera dragged down the passage behind him, dark, waking in the pile at three hearts behind private packed carcasses torn loose by blows; ten seconds' grace, then he hunts again. Drag and burial clocks are in memory; the blow's caller marks the reader's own record copy. Child's room: the ceiling holds mobiles the room made of the toys it took (bears, paper-doll garland, alphabet blocks, planets), upright on strings, not flipped toys. 52 sources / 39 resolutions / two kinds / three endings. October 18 owner request, candidate 0.4.104 (on 0.4.103): rework Winter Lake as the dare, the winter after the Shallows. WinterLake builds the lake whole (polish, composition, review passes and StaceyCover skip it): near bank with the boys' fire, sled (account beside it), stump with Bud's one-page note, lantern, snow fort and snowball heaps; ice at y -1 over five blocks of water in winter_prop kinds (black ice clear, rings and slips; snow ice opaque and quiet; cracked; split; frozen cattails; snowballs), the hole that never froze at (0,-46) with a faint light under it, a plank-floored shanty, spruce banks. Bud, Hank, Clay and Lonnie (stranger phases 34-37, boy scale 0.8, painter winter mark: knit cap, scarf, mittens; catalog 136, still 23 views) are shared: snowballs until Hank stops them at 900 occupied ticks; Hank's third word gives the reader her owner-bound ribbon (winter_ribbon). She is one adopted body under the ice (LakeWitchEntity drive/underIce, synced, drawn upside down and faintly lit), moved by WinterLake by sound: running 3, walking 1, crouching/still 0, black ice x1.5, snow x0.4, nothing off the ice; snowballs landing on the ice draw her; suspicion under a noisy reader knocks (20/40), cracks (60/80) and breaks through at 100, pulling them under for one heart, then she rests 160 ticks; holes have their own light. Using the ribbon within 3.5 of the hole: a hand takes it (60 ticks), the boys run once per night (fire out, hidden after 100 ticks); ready on the near bank. Night resets after 100 empty, unwatched ticks, mending every crack. Witness title "The dare", outcome and survival kind kept. Seven subtitled sounds (tools/generate_winter_lake_audio.py), art by tools/draw_winter_lake_art.py. Stacey's general cases keep their bare Winter Lake frames (the controller acts only on built scenes and on the body it adopts); the cover case moves to Movie Night; the old ice-break case is retired; three cases added; three native Winter Lake views after Costume Night's. Protocol 76, version 0.4.104, new builds only. 46 sources / 35 resolutions / two kinds / three endings unchanged. Run 39099171239 (95e2abd): the costume still case measured her end position after she went back to the spot she started from; it now measures how far she came. Run 39101258901 (e7c7a99): eleven of twelve jobs passed, including literary (all three Winter Lake cases) and live-multiplayer (a connected client at the lake now sees midnight). The build job failed on two checks that had never run: Winter Lake's source is a lectern now that the review pass skips it (added to the architecture lectern list), and Movie Night's view check compared the canoe hull, measured in blocks, against 38 pixels (now 2.4 blocks). The Ghosts upstairs-take case also failed once there, on code nothing here touches. Old Hank at Costume Night now remembers a reader who did the dare. October 18 owner request, candidate 0.4.103 (on 0.4.102): rework Costume Night as Proofrock's Halloween Lake Witch costume contest. CostumeNight builds the beach whole (polish, composition and review passes skip it; armor stands, the shoreline pass and StaceyCover rocks are retired here): judges' table with Hank's card at the entrance, a plank stage with backdrop and contest board, bonfire with hay bales and a radio, apple tub, candy pail, pumpkins, four light poles strung with coloured bulbs, the lake along the east side, and the town park's grass (the only grass) beyond low posts at the north end, where the account waits. Thirteen skins on stranger phases 21-33 (Hank, the boy from the Shallows, and twelve numbered entrants, four children, five who crawl from the start), painted by tools/paint_character_skins.py with new costume features (yarn and mop wigs over the face, masks, face paint, pillowcase, hood, bags, weeds, gown, flowered dress, number cards); catalog +13, character art views 0..22. One shared occupied clock: Hank calls a number every 240 ticks from 120 (the entrant walks or crawls to the stage front, shows off, goes back); at 900 he gives the ribbon to "the one down by the water"; 260 ticks later the bulbs pop and go dark, the radio dies and every entrant crawls; reset after 100 empty ticks with no camera within 70 blocks. Her rule here alone (LakeWitchEntity.tickCostume): she moves only while no living reader there can see her, never moves while watched or while the beach is empty, only shifts between four water's-edge places before lights out and never strikes then; after lights out she comes for the nearest reader on the sand at .4 a tick, strikes unseen for four after a five-tick windup, then returns to the water for 80 ticks; never on grass, never within ten blocks of the entrance; drips (particles and a subtitled drip). Ready on the park grass once the lights are out, she has been seen and the reader has been out on the sand since (hints otherwise). Hank's card source, account "The account: the ribbon" (thirteenth witch, Hank's story if told, the Shallows look if thrown, the ribbon, the crawl, struck; "one of them was not playing"), Witness title "The thirteenth witch" and new line; outcome and survival kind kept. costume_prop block (48 variants, tools/draw_costume_night_art.py), six subtitled sounds (tools/generate_costume_night_audio.py), crawl gesture 5 (1.25 high). Protocol 75, version 0.4.103, new builds only. Stacey's ten general cases, two scene-review witch cases and the live door break move to Winter Lake (the strike case drops its grass check); the costume-stand case is retired; three cases added; three native Costume Night views after Movie Night's. 46 sources / 35 resolutions / two kinds / three endings unchanged. Scene clocks never reached the sky (the client hooked getDayTime but the sky, sun and daylight read dayTime); HouseSceneSkyMixin routes dayTime through the same clock, so Movie Night is at night and every scene shows its own hour; the live proof checks midnight at the lake on a connected client. Run 39085973986 (2fba9ed): the ribbon case checked Tina home after Hank had called number two; it now checks only that her own call is over. Validation pending. Canon: docs/COSTUME_NIGHT_0_4_103.md.

October 17 owner request, same candidate 0.4.102: people and custom textures for Movie Night. MovieNightCast: Ray the projectionist on the dock, Dot behind the ticket-hut counter, the Pruitts (Frank, June, the boy Jamie at child scale) in lawn chairs on the sand, and Bobby and Lynn in a teal canoe tied to a buoy by the screen (riders only for them). Skins literary_movie_* on stranger phases 14-20, painted by tools/paint_character_skins.py (catalog +7, character art views 0..20). They talk on right-click, speak idle lines and react to set moments of the film. One shared clock counts while the scene is occupied: the boy is due at 600 ticks, the teenagers at 1100, Frank at 1600 and June at 2200. Each goes only in the dark (never lit by a burst) and only when no living player or spectator in the level can see them, and leaves a splash, the boy's spilled popcorn, Frank's tipped chair, or the empty teen canoe drifting. The family's and Ray's words change with who is gone. Dot (never taken) greets each reader once and asks after the Pruitts. Readers present when someone vanishes get Missing0502 bits; the account opens with the audience, names only those who went missing in that reader's night and Dot's words, and ends "for nobody" only once June is gone. When the scene has been empty 100 ticks with no camera within 70 blocks, everyone is put back. Textures, all drawn by tools/draw_movie_night_art.py: movie_material (dock, raft, pier, canvas, frame, siding; her footing is now the dock and raft boards, not the pier's cedar; the screen block uses the canvas); eleven new movie_prop kinds (56 variants) with models (lawn chair and tipped, blanket, cooler, popcorn and spilled, poster halves, life ring, film cans, buoy); five canoe paints. LakeCanoeEntity paint (synced, saved), with LakeCanoeRenderer drawing paint 0 as the vanilla boat and other paints on the lake house canoe hull (now generic). Rebuilt beach, dock, screen, barge, pier and hut (counter moved to z -86). One new case (cast and vanishing, 657 declared); the canoe-count cases count only the four dock canoes; the footing case uses the boards; nine native Movie Night views. Validation pending. Canon: docs/MOVIE_NIGHT_0_4_102.md.

October 17 owner request, candidate 0.4.102 (on the 0.4.101 Shallows and Holy Rabbit candidate): rework Movie Night as a freer adaptation, with a film on the screen, a rare harmless shark, and the fireworks, speakers and pier in play. MovieNight builds the scene whole (polish, composition and review passes skip it; StaceyCover's rocks stay): a beach, a dock with the projector and four canoes, a screen on a raft mid-lake, a firework barge, and a spruce far pier with a boardwalk to the ticket hut where the account waits. *Teeth of Summer* (original, 13 shots, 2600 ticks, keyed to the level clock) is composed per tick by MovieFilm from an atlas drawn in code (tools/draw_movie_night_art.py) and drawn by MovieScreenRenderer with burned-in captions, an additive beam that writes no depth, and the shadows of readers and of her in the beam. Bursts go up every 60-100 ticks at each rocket's own lifetime and flash the whole lake; while lit she does not move; in the dark she stalks at half pace; looking turns her back only in the beam; she keeps to water and the grey dock/barge boards or the black raft, never the pier's cedar or the banks, and hunts only readers in canoes, in the water or out on those boards. Exposures keep the old rule (seen in a burst, in clear view, past the beach). The score plays per reader from the two nearest speakers and cuts out within 12 blocks of her in the dark, resuming past 16 or in a burst. About one visit in three, once, a private, unsaved, collisionless shark circles that reader's canoe and goes under it (a camera rock and a knock). Four canoes, one to a reader, each returning to its place; landing at the pier ends the scene with 30 blocks rowed and two exposures. Program, account and Witness line rewritten; nine subtitled sounds (tools/generate_movie_night_audio.py); protocol 74, layout 45, new builds only. Three cases rewritten and three added; native visual views (see the cast paragraph above for the current counts). Validation pending. Canon: docs/MOVIE_NIGHT_0_4_102.md.

October 17 owner request, same candidate 0.4.101: a pass on the Holy Rabbit after the gameplay and storytelling review. A committed reader can no longer be trapped: the trail's escape cold starts only once Father's retreat has finished (0.4.90), resets under the tree and allows a minute; Father waits, pointing, until the reader has seen Slaney before the apparent harvest, and a morning counts any time before he takes him (all day on the last); the carving appears on the last day the first moment nobody is looking, Father points to it once, and it enriches the account instead of gating the way out (meals, mornings, wound and his going, per 0.4.88). The cold and Father's shaking never take the last three hearts (native frost held just short of fully frozen there). Story: the source no longer gives the ending away and speaks in the child's words; the name is given once; the wound is shown, not explained; the account names the reader's rabbit, mentions the carving only if read, and ends on its own line under "The food in the hollow". Owner follow-ups: new builds open the canopy cell in front of the trunk's carved words; the Holy Rabbit is now an understanding ending (was survival); mid-save edge cases are out of scope (unreleased mod). No actor, item or sound change. 653 native cases (three added). Validation pending. Canon: docs/HOLY_RABBIT_PASS_0_4_101.md.

October 17 owner request, candidate 0.4.101 (on 0.4.100 4f10828): rework the Shallows. The reader takes the girl's wrists (the witch's human skin) while a private boy, Lonnie, holds her feet; the reader's strafe keys swing her out over the water and back, one stroke per press; Lonnie counts the strokes out and on three both let go mid-swing. The three saved bank boys (original UUIDs; Hank and Clay get their own painted faces, Lonnie a fourth) tease her about being a witch and react, privately to each reader. She lands on the lake's surface without going under, breaks (limbs twisted), lies still 3.5 s, gets up wrong and runs north across the water into the woods on all fours. The landing is the throwing beat: essays, Witness (threw_her) and exhaustion for that reader only. Sneaking, dropping the grip or leaving lets go and restarts the count; a blow before the throw still darkens the visit. Two readers get separate carry places. No geometry change: layout 45 / protocol 73; 46 sources / 35 resolutions / two kinds / three endings. # Vignette updates

Previous 0.4.42 polishes every scene once (ScenePolish, checkpoint scene_polish_0442; in place for existing worlds, one vacant scene at a time): hung lanterns, wall fittings on walls, no unsupported blocks, rooted trees, persistent authored leaves, unburied creatures, wooded outdoor borders instead of void edges, and lit interiors except those dark by design. SceneDressing furnishes measured-bare rooms and flat outdoor ground by theme, never in story volumes, doorways or passages under three wide. Vanilla monsters stay out of the manor and impossible hallway. Every spot keeps a way on: arrival opens an ordinary onward door when none leads on (stories behind an only door are kept, loops are not), and a blocked dealt door reroutes onward when nothing else does. The great staircase is offered only at depth 20+ (the return stack now remembers 32 doors). Layout 33 appends stone halls (STONE_GALLERY/CROSSING/DESCENT, slots 69–71) that become the ordinary ways on from depth 12 while domestic halls fade out by 14; isLiterary is slots 45–68. VignetteAudit publishes a report-only polish audit as CI annotations. Canon: docs/POLISH_AND_PROGRESS_0_4_42.md.

Current 0.4.41 repairs shared doors. Labyrinth entry doors shut once per crossing and never while another player is within three blocks; arrival vestibule copies skip cells other players occupy. Exterior pass-close leaves the door to the idle closer when another player is at it; walking out an open exterior door is DOOR, not BREACH. BREACH needs three consecutive invalid ticks and logs which House volumes rejected the player. Exterior door grace is 5.25 blocks (native reach) plus a per-player handle-crossing grace; hallway returns clamp inside its walls and blocked matched returns land in front of the player's own door. Preserve 0.4.40 lazy section buffers and depth-tiered carve. The seam namespace declares four cases; SharedDoorTests joins the default suite. Canon: docs/SHARED_DOORS_0_4_41.md. Arrivals use a saved per-player route map (LabyrinthDealer.arriveAt): the same route restores the same doors, new spots are seeded from route and manor so shared routes share halls, and only closed destinations, scent or pet rescue re-deal; ordinary deals never ping-pong back to the previous hall. Canon: docs/LABYRINTH_MAP_0_4_41.md. The revealed impossible hallway box is shielded from fire (common FireBlockMixin), explosions and mob block-breaking, and mends burnt shell cells every ten seconds outside GameTests.

Current 0.4.39 repairs the exit seam. The held client frame releases after its existing 1.5-second deadline once the destination player/level exist, even if the vanilla readiness supplier remains false. Keep the early surroundings-ready release. Authoritative mirrored states use UPDATE_KNOWN_SHAPE and UPDATE_SUPPRESS_DROPS so copying door halves before their support does not destroy them. A live PendingDoorClose protects its door from the forty-tick idle closer; normal passage/distance/timeout closure remains. Preserve layout 32 / protocol 32 and every 0.4.38 repair, original property and personal outcome. Require all 323 native gameplay cases, two focused native seam cases, three hallway cases, sixteen literary cases, seven hotel cases and all existing package/writing/texture/client/architecture/sky proofs. A focused candidate is not complete-suite verification. Record client capture/held-frame and server sync/move timings; do not infer a server stall from a gap in unrelated log messages. Canon: docs/SEAM_EXIT_0_4_39.md.

The 0.4.38 continuation indexes recorded state reads by chunk, preserving authored order. Keep saved cursors, original source equipment/property and finite copy protection. NativeTestChunks leases are fixture-only and close after the old-layout append's original actor/container assertions. The native home-copy test waits for genuine completion within its existing timeout and verifies capture-ticket release; do not replace its physical, custody, multiplayer or two architecture-view checks with mocks.

Version 0.4.28 reshapes vignette shells and outdoor edges in place; layout 26, protocol 28. `SceneShells` runs once per scene after its furnishings (checkpoint scene_shells_0428, cleared only by an explicit rebuild): it may only re-material a room's own wall, ceiling and floor planes or carve into fill or empty space behind them, never doors, block entities, supports of attached blocks or entities, story volumes, the copied vestibule or walkable cells. Keep outdoor edges physical: no barrier walls around the plain or courtyard, dunes rise in steps of two or more, and `OutdoorBounds` only returns players a scene has seen standing inside it. Verify the architecture GameTest's shell, window, fireplace, dune, barrier and bounds checks.

October 2 source-informed design is approved in docs/DESIGN_DOCUMENT.md. Ordinary unfinished visits allow retreat; only explicit committed beats lock exits. Preserve personal dormant sources/rediscovery and save actual retreat, domestic and companion facts without granting Witness or inventing history. Letters and echoes must remain per-reader, finite and based on native actions. The visible caged child and combat creature share a UUID, but no actual observer may watch their change; preserve the peaceful release and living wounded creature. The staircase now descends 1,280 blocks to ARENA=92 and continues another 1,280 below, within the expanded interior dimension; layout 25 / protocol 28. Update design status and native evidence as each part is implemented.

Hide-and-clap reserves on entry, but its commitment is actual blindfold equipment. Native departure/death/offline absence before starting clears only the reservation; original props and finite inventory remain intact. Once started, retain the native binding, deadline, reveal and saved pause. Verify this alongside actual survival-player Witch strikes, spectator-aware prisoner occlusion, stable spatial scene clocks and both halves of the real staircase.

When adding or changing a vignette, follow [docs/VIGNETTE_RELEASE_CHECKLIST.md](docs/VIGNETTE_RELEASE_CHECKLIST.md). Review its personal Witness resolution, shipped story registration, resulting threshold, tests and current documentation as part of the same change.

Witness requires 75% of the playable, eligible story pool, rounded up, across at least two story kinds. Keep the quota derived from `WitnessAccount.Story`, rather than freezing it to a number. Only fully playable sources with an actual personal resolution belong in that pool. Separate choices within one vignette count once. Preserve stored evidence and completed endings when growing the pool.

Previous 0.4.30 baseline: seventeen eligible stories, thirteen required resolutions, three ending options. Update these documented counts with each vignette release. Phone in the Canoe is a personal memory source. Its completed clip grants credit once; its original phone is recovered from the preserved cave.
